smart__CodeSystem__KGGrcStatus__elements__nameKGGrcStatus
smart__CodeSystem__KGGrcStatus__elements__titleKnowledge graph L1: grc status
smart__CodeSystem__KGGrcStatus__elements__descriptionWhether the Guideline Review Committee approved the publication. All WHO publications containing recommendations must be approved (handbook §1.10.1); a consolidated guideline whose recommendations were all previously approved and unchanged does not require review (§1.7.2). Absent means not recorded — not the same as not reviewed. Source: smart-kg ontology/l1/l1.json valueSets "grc-status"; WHO handbook for guideline development (2014), §1.7.2, §1.10.1.

smart__CodeSystem__KGPublicationType__elements__nameKGPublicationType
smart__CodeSystem__KGPublicationType__elements__titleKnowledge graph L1: publication type
smart__CodeSystem__KGPublicationType__elements__descriptionWhat kind of publication this is. The distinction that matters most for provenance is guideline versus not: a summary table or position paper restates recommendations made elsewhere, and a §1.9 product makes none of its own. Conflating them makes a citation resolve to the wrong authority. Source: smart-kg ontology/l1/l1.json valueSets "publication-type"; WHO handbook for guideline development (2014), Table 1.2 and §1.7–1.9; the BCG decision table's citation for summary-table; CDHIv2.fsh for classification.

smart__CodeSystem__KGRecommendationKind__elements__descriptionWhat sort of normative statement this is. Grading is optional: ANC 2016 prints a direction ('Recommended', 'Not recommended') and no GRADE strength. A strength always needs a direction. A good practice statement and a no-recommendation carry no strength or certainty, and a no-recommendation carries no direction. Source: smart-kg ontology/l1/l1.json valueSets "recommendation-kind"; WHO handbook §10.4, §10.7; ANC guideline (2016) Table 1, verified; Guyatt et al. 2016 on good practice statements.

smart__ConceptMap__CDHIv1toCDHIv2__elements__descriptionMapping from the Classification of Digital Health Interventions v1 (CDHI v1, 2018) to the Classification of Digital Interventions, Services and Applications in Health v2 (CDISAH v2, 2023). Key structural changes reflected in this map: - User group labels updated throughout (e.g. 'Clients' → 'Persons', 'Health workers' → 'Healthcare providers', 'Health system managers' → 'Health management and support personnel'). - Civil Registration and Vital Statistics (CRVS) consolidated: six v1 codes (3.4.1–3.4.6) merged into two v2 codes (3.4.1, 3.4.2). - Health financing section restructured: v1 3.5.1 (insurance membership) and 3.5.2 (billing) updated; v1 3.5.3–3.5.6 shifted by one (now 3.5.4–3.5.6 + new 3.5.3). - Data services (group 4) substantially revised: 4.1.1 changed scope, 4.3 expanded from 4 to 5 codes, 4.4 split from 1 to 3 codes, 4.5 is entirely new. - New v2 categories with no v1 equivalent are listed as 'unmatched' targets.

smart__ConceptMap__CDSCv1toCDSCv2__elements__nameCDSCv1toCDSCv2
smart__ConceptMap__CDSCv1toCDSCv2__elements__titleMapping from CDSC v1 to Services and Application Types v2
smart__ConceptMap__CDSCv1toCDSCv2__elements__descriptionMapping from the Classification of Digital Health System Categories v1 (CDSCv1, 2018) to the Classification of Digital Health Services and Application Types v2 (CDSCv2, 2023). The v1 used 25 single-letter codes (A–Y). The v2 completely restructured this into 5 representations within the digital health enterprise architecture, each with alphanumeric codes (A1–A9, B1–B8, C1–C11, D1–D8, E1–E2). Several new v2 categories have no v1 equivalent: A3 (Decision support), A4 (Diagnostics), B1 (Blood bank), B3 (Health program monitoring), B7 (Patient administration), C4 (Facility registries), C5 (Health worker registry), C7 (Immunisation information), C8 (Master patient index), C9 (Product catalogues), C10 (Public Key directories), D1 (Analytics), D3 (Data warehouses).
smart__ConceptMap__CDSCv1toCDSCv2__elements__statusdraft
smart__ConceptMap__CDSCv1toCDSCv2__elements__version0.3.0
smart__ConceptMap__CDSCv1toCDSCv2__elements__date2026-11-23T18:43:35+00:00

smart__StructureDefinition__KGCqlLibrary__elements__descriptionA .cql file on disk, as opposed to the FHIR Library resource that wraps it. A node so that the mapping table's four CQL rows -- CQL Concepts and CQL Data Elements under Elements, CQL under Decision Tables, CQL IndicatorLogic under Indicators -- do not dead-end. smart__StructureDefinition__KGDecisionSupportLogic__elements__titleKnowledge graph L2: Decision-Support Logic
smart__StructureDefinition__KGDecisionSupportLogic__elements__descriptionDAK component 6. The whole model is an id, an optional description and `source 1..1 uri` to a DMN file. Nothing about the decision itself -- which is the gap the l2-dmn subgraph fills. resolutionStatus is `unresolved` on a component that something else names but no DAK declares -- an L3 artefact whose title carries the component id, for instance. The node exists so the gap is queryable and carries the referenced id and nothing else. Class IRI: http://smart.who.int/kg/decision-support-logic.

smart__StructureDefinition__KGImplementationPractice__elements__nameKGImplementationPractice
smart__StructureDefinition__KGImplementationPractice__elements__titleKnowledge graph L1-LIBRARY: Implementation practice
smart__StructureDefinition__KGImplementationPractice__elements__descriptionA statement of how to put guidance into practice, printed in a publication whose publicationType is implementation-guidance or methodology: the handbook's "products that are not guidelines" (§1.9), a "how to" document that makes no recommendations of its own. Not a recommendation, and not a good practice statement — both are made by a guideline and approved by the GRC; this is neither. It carries no kind, direction, strength or certainty, because nothing graded it. A handbook's own "Best practices" lists are the typical source. Owner ruling 1b, 2026-10-12: model these as their own class, not as recommendation nodes of a new kind. statement is verbatim and is the only stored copy, as recommendation's is. identifier is used in the IRI. There is no remark: L1 3.0's remark is minted under a recommendation (its iriPattern) and hasRemark starts at one, so a remark of a practice needs L1 itself to change — proposed upstream, not patched here. Class IRI: http://smart.who.int/kg/implementation-practice.

smart__StructureDefinition__KGIndicator__elements__descriptionA WHO indicator, identified by its published reference number. Corresponds to ProgramIndicator: definition, numerator, denominator and disaggregation are ProgramIndicator's elements and types (markdown); not a Parent because ProgramIndicator makes them 1..1 and an L1 indicator is recorded with what its source prints. Class IRI: http://smart.who.int/kg/indicator.

smart__StructureDefinition__KGPersona__elements__nameKGPersona
smart__StructureDefinition__KGPersona__elements__titleKnowledge graph L2: Generic Persona
smart__StructureDefinition__KGPersona__elements__descriptionDAK component 2. Grounded in GenericPersona.fsh -- title, id, description, otherNames, iscoCode bound to ISCO08ValueSet -- and in the 22 committed ActorDefinition instances that carry name, title, type and description. The two disagree about where ISCO lives, which the properties record rather than smooth over. `title` is the human-readable label a BPMN pool matches on; `name` is the CamelCase identifier a Canonical() reference matches on. They are different strings for the same persona and conflating them is precisely how the pool join breaks. GenericPersona.fsh declares `iscoCode 0..* code`, but the committed ActorDefinitions record ISCO in prose inside description, so iscoCode is usually empty and is not authoritative. Class IRI: http://smart.who.int/kg/persona.

smart__StructureDefinition__KGProgramIndicator__elements__nameKGProgramIndicator
smart__StructureDefinition__KGProgramIndicator__elements__titleKnowledge graph L2: Program Indicator
smart__StructureDefinition__KGProgramIndicator__elements__descriptionDAK component 7. Named program-indicator and not `indicator` because L1 already declares an `indicator` -- one normatively defined in a publication. They are different things: this is the DAK's operational measure, and `derivedFrom` is how it points at the normative one. Collapsing them would silently merge a WHO definition with a country's implementation of it. numerator, denominator and disaggregation are all `1..1 markdown` -- prose, not a computable expression. Nothing here should be read as a calculable measure; the L3 FHIR Measure is where that would live. resolutionStatus is `unresolved` on a component that something else names but no DAK declares -- an L3 artefact whose title carries the component id, for instance. The node exists so the gap is queryable and carries the referenced id and nothing else. Class IRI: http://smart.who.int/kg/program-indicator.

smart__StructureDefinition__KGPublication__elements__nameKGPublication
smart__StructureDefinition__KGPublication__elements__titleKnowledge graph L1: Publication
smart__StructureDefinition__KGPublication__elements__descriptionA WHO publication: a guideline of any of the handbook's types, a position paper, a summary table, a classification, implementation guidance, or a supplement. Metadata follows Dublin Core. identifiers is a list of {type, value}; type is from identifier-type, and the first of isbn, iris-handle, doi, issn, url builds the IRI, so an edition change is a new publication. issued and modified are Dublin Core. reviewBy is the handbook's review-by date (§12.5.1). sha256 pins the PDF. Derives from DublinCore (smart-base input/fsh/models/DublinCore.fsh); title, creator, publisher, language, rights are its elements. Class IRI: http://smart.who.int/kg/publication.

smart__StructureDefinition__KGPublicationElement__elements__nameKGPublicationElement
smart__StructureDefinition__KGPublicationElement__elements__titleKnowledge graph L1: Publication element
smart__StructureDefinition__KGPublicationElement__elements__descriptionOne printed block: a table, a table row, a footnote, a figure, a chart, an image, a flowchart, a box or a list. It records where something is printed and exactly what is printed that no content node holds. It carries no meaning of its own; meaning lives in content nodes, joined by presentedIn. columns is the header text, verbatim. columnMap names, per column, the content field that fills it when the table is re-rendered (e.g. 'Recommendation' -> statement). cells holds only text no content node holds; a cell filled through columnMap is null. text is for unmodelled printed text such as a footnote that is not a remark. sha256 is for images. Class IRI: http://smart.who.int/kg/publication-element.

smart__StructureDefinition__KGRecommendation__elements__titleKnowledge graph L1: Recommendation
smart__StructureDefinition__KGRecommendation__elements__descriptionA normative statement from a WHO guideline. Kinds other than a graded recommendation (context-specific, research-only, good practice statement, no-recommendation) are the same node with a different kind. statement is verbatim and is the only stored copy. intervention, population, setting, provider and timing are verbatim slots taken from the statement, its remarks, or the enclosing caption or heading. setting absorbs the former conditionality. identifier is the published number (e.g. A.1.1), used in the IRI; sub-recommendations append their letter. Class IRI: http://smart.who.int/kg/recommendation.

smart__StructureDefinition__KGRequirementStatement__link__html<a href="StructureDefinition-KGRequirementStatement.html">Knowledge graph L2: Requirement Statement</a>
smart__StructureDefinition__KGRequirementStatement__elements__nameKGRequirementStatement
smart__StructureDefinition__KGRequirementStatement__elements__titleKnowledge graph L2: Requirement Statement
smart__StructureDefinition__KGRequirementStatement__elements__descriptionOne numbered, labelled statement inside a requirement, with a conformance verb. A node rather than a property because conformance is queried across a whole DAK -- 'every SHALL in this kit, and what fulfils it' is the question requirements exist to answer. It also carries its own success criteria, so 'what would show this SHALL is met' is answerable per statement rather than per requirement. conformance is the FHIR ConformanceExpectation verb: SHALL, SHOULD, MAY or SHOULD-NOT. Across the 41 committed instances it is 155 SHALL and 15 SHOULD. successCriteria is a LIST of objects, each {key, criterion, verification}: `key` is unique within the statement (e.g. SC-1), `criterion` is a checkable sentence, and `verification` is one of test | inspection | review | analysis -- the same field name and enum as folio-assistant's requirement.ts `successCriteria`, so the two map 1:1. smart-base types it, so the shape is enforced by the schema; a statement with no successCriteria is still valid (the 170 statements committed in smart-base carry none). Class IRI: http://smart.who.int/kg/requirement-statement.

smart__StructureDefinition__KGSchedulingLogic__elements__nameKGSchedulingLogic
smart__StructureDefinition__KGSchedulingLogic__elements__titleKnowledge graph L2: Scheduling Logic
smart__StructureDefinition__KGSchedulingLogic__elements__descriptionWhen an intervention is due, as a DAK component. NOT declared by DAK.fsh -- it is added here because the published SOP lists Scheduling Logic as an active L2 input (spreadsheets under input/scheduling-logic), WHO's ArchiMate model declares it a BusinessObject realised by PlanDefinition, and L1's schedules needed an L2 component to reach (since L1 3.0, a recommendation's timing slot). Note that the SOP's own L3 row for scheduling is present but HTML-commented-out and there is no l3_schedulinglogic.md page, so WHO has drafted the mapping and not activated it; the L3 edge is licensed here as a deliberate departure, recorded rather than hidden. resolutionStatus is `unresolved` on a component that something else names but no DAK declares -- an L3 artefact whose title carries the component id, for instance. The node exists so the gap is queryable and carries the referenced id and nothing else. Class IRI: http://smart.who.int/kg/scheduling-logic.

smart__StructureDefinition__KGTerminologyCode__elements__nameKGTerminologyCode
smart__StructureDefinition__KGTerminologyCode__elements__titleKnowledge graph L1: Terminology code
smart__StructureDefinition__KGTerminologyCode__elements__descriptionA code in an external terminology — ICD-10, ICD-11, SNOMED CT, ATC, or a WHO classification such as CDHI. CROSS-REFERENCE ONLY. It records system, code and display, and asserts NOTHING about the terminology: no hierarchy, no subsumption, no synonyms, no post-coordination. The terminology has its own authority, its own release cycle and its own tooling, and a partial copy here would be wrong within one release. Resolve meaning against the terminology server, not against this graph. Derives from Coding (FHIR R4 core datatype); system, code, display, version are its elements. Class IRI: http://smart.who.int/kg/terminology-code.
